   - Original goal (earlier context): alon, project alpha_heroes, a superhuman Heroes 3 AI on VCMI with PyTorch battle and campaign models, PPO and joint training. The ask was: "improve performance... I want to train fast... tell me tiny operations with huge cost... You may improve the engine (without changing what it actually does)". Other machines play games for the master GPU machine, an RTX 5090 PC (Ryzen 9 9900X, WSL2 capped at 20 GB RAM, where RAM is the limit).
   - This session, in order:
     - "push". Main was pushed.
     - "then see if there is anything else that can be improved. note that another AI is working on the buisness logic now and you should not interfere with it".
     - "yes push, if these are safe pure preformace improvements just push them. Be careful though of code that was changed by the other AI and is still being tested". Only my performance commit was pushed, as 0239df9.
     - "can you do that without affecting buisness logic? can you do it without killing the current run?" This was about 4 leftover items: evals stopping training; campaign record compression; heroes' paths recomputed; legal-only action heads.
     - "the other AI stopped, you can do what you need 1 and 4 probably and commit and push to main." Item 1 was done and pushed. Item 4 was measured and deliberately not done, with the reason given.
   - Standing rules (CLAUDE.md and memory):
     - Push back plainly and be concise.
     - AWS only in us-west-2/us-west-2a.
     - Cap every data-generating run (time, disk, stall); report MB and MB/min; never leave processes running; kill by PID; use scripts/headless.sh.
     - Minimal engine changes; label each patch (bug fix vs behaviour change vs performance).
     - Python owns the science.
     - Decide clear calls, recommend on balanced ones.
     - LR never above 1e-4.
     - No GitHub Actions (strip .github workflows before pushing third-party code).
     - Commit or push only when the user asks.
     - Do not interfere with the other AI's (b1's) business-logic files; be careful with its code still under test.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Hosts and infrastructure:
     - VCMI headless clients, including the persistent BattleProcess queue (TestQueue: max_battles=50, then restart).
     - AlphaBattleAI and AlphaMapAI.
     - Inference servers on CPU on the Mac and CUDA on the PC.
     - ActorPool with pause/resume; CampaignLearner with UpdateProcess.
   - Measuring:
     - On a shared machine, measure with instructions retired: a `/usr/bin/time -l` wrapper set through `VCMI_CLIENT` (timed_client.sh).
     - In-process ONNX battles are dominated by ORT session creation (~1.5 s; cached per process in production).
     - `sample`, `footprint`, `vm_stat`, `heap`, `vmmap` for profiling.
   - PyTorch MPS allocator: its low watermark is 1.4 × the recommended 17.76 GB ≈ 24.9 GB per process. `PYTORCH_MPS_LOW_WATERMARK_RATIO=0.2` is a soft limit and works even when set after `import torch`.
   - VCMI spells: `BattleSpellMechanics::canBeCastAt` calls `canBeCast`, which is target-independent and costs about 95% of the check.
   - Git: worktrees with submodules (`--reference`), ccache keyed on absolute paths, `git merge-tree --write-tree` for merge dry-runs, a no-op merge to reconcile a cherry-picked commit.
   - Cross-session coordination with peer b1 (alpha-heroes-b1, address uds:/tmp/cc-socks/24608.sock) via SendMessage.

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **vcmi/lib/spells/BattleSpellMechanics.{h,cpp}** (vcmi commit 3102490f2, pushed to ARozental/vcmi alpha_heroes). New public non-virtual `canBeCastAtIfCastable(const Target&)` plus an overload taking `Problem&`. `canBeCastAt(target, problem)` is now `if(!canBeCast(problem)) return false; return canBeCastAtIfCastable(target, problem);`. Labeled "Performance (alpha_heroes, no behaviour change)".
   - **agents/AlphaBattleAI/Encoder.cpp**:
     - Includes `lib/spells/BattleSpellMechanics.h`.
     - New helper `canCastAt(mechanics, castable, target)`: it dynamic_casts to BattleSpellMechanics and otherwise falls back to `canBeCastAt`.
     - `castAffects(mechanics, bool castable, target, unit)` takes the castable flag.
     - In `encodeSpellMasks` and `encodeCreatureSpellTargets`, `castable = mechanics->canBeCast(problem)` is computed once per spell.
   - **agents/AlphaBattleAI/Dump.{cpp,h}**: BattleRecord now stores `std::vector<std::string> decisionBytes` (one string per decision, inputs as int32 via memcpy or bool bytes, then float32 outputs) and `tensorBytes`. `npyHeader()` is factored out of `npy()`. `serializeInto(std::string&)` reserves the total size first and writes each tensor's bytes once; `send()` uses `serializeInto(message)`. It throws if a tensor's size changes within a record.
   - **battle_model/device.py**: `os.environ.setdefault("PYTORCH_MPS_LOW_WATERMARK_RATIO", "0.2")`, with a comment.
   - **battle_model/train.py** (commit f0b5c75):
     - `class EvalStopped(Exception)`.
     - `play_eval(..., stop=None)`: workers break when `stop` is set, then it raises `EvalStopped`.
     - `eval_round(..., name="", stop=None)`.
     - `evaluate_now(epoch, scored=None, live_weights=None, stop=None)`.
     - `evaluate_against(epoch, config_for, baseline, scored, stop=None)`.
     - New `BackgroundEval` class inside `train()`:
       - `running()`.
       - `start()` prints "eval N playing beside training" and runs a thread that catches EvalStopped (printing "stopped with the run: its unplayed rounds have no row, and it no total") and stores other errors.
       - `finished` holds a timestamp.
       - `join()`, and `close()` (stop.set plus a join with timeout 600).
     - Loop logic:
       - raise `evals.error` if set;
       - `idle = not evals.running()`, and take `last_eval` from `evals.finished`;
       - `due = idle and ... if config.eval_minutes else epoch % eval_every == 0`;
       - an `eval_pending` flag;
       - when pending and idle, `save_checkpoint` first, then `evals.start(epoch, cpu_copy(average.model if average else model), cpu_copy(model) if average and average.updates and config.eval_live else None)` if `pool` exists, else a synchronous `evaluate_now`;
       - campaign evals require `not evals.running()` and still pause the pool;
       - `evals.join()` after the loop and `evals.close()` first in `finally`.
     - The eval_workers comment was updated.
   - **docs/performance_2026-10-29.md**:
     - "Second round (2026-10-30)" section: the MPS thrash, the spells and record fixes with numbers, the campaign log spam.
     - Entries for the background battle evals and the CPU-inference profile.
     - A "Not done from this round" list.
     - A corrected recommendation: CPU inference is half the features and a fifth the heads.
   - **Memory**: `memory/perf-2026-10-29.md` gained second- and third-round summaries; the MEMORY.md index line was updated.
   - **Scratchpad** (/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alon-projects-alpha-heroes/31529395-f677-4318-b857-085c1c3696a2/scratchpad):
     - Run and profile: joint_ab.py (with cwd_of guard), mem_prof.py, vmrate.sh.
     - Old-vs-new build comparisons: timed_client.sh, replay_ab.py, bench_ab.py (both swap build/).
     - Small checks: one_battle.py, campaign_zlib.py, capture.py (records.pkl), prof_cpu.py, prof_facts.py, eval_check.py, mps_cache.py, children.py, inclusive.py.

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **Stray-client abort:** joint_ab aborted because b1 had a vcmiclient running. Fixed by turning the abort into a note and limiting orphan kills to clients whose cwd is under my root.
   - **Sleep blocked:** `sleep N; cat` was blocked by the harness. I used run_in_background until-loops instead.
   - **Wide rebuild:** adding a virtual method to ISpellMechanics.h would force a huge rebuild. Reverted; the method is non-virtual on BattleSpellMechanics, reached through dynamic_cast.
   - **replay_ab errors:**
     - ModuleNotFoundError: fixed with PYTHONPATH=.
     - The map build failed after build/ was renamed: the maps are now generated before the swap.
   - **campaign_zlib.py:** failed for lack of a `__main__` guard (spawn); added one.
   - **Battle-client measurement:** instruction counts were swamped by ORT session creation in per-battle processes. I switched to bench_ab with persistent processes and the inference server.
   - **No `timeout` on macOS:** used a background `sleep 900; kill -TERM` watchdog instead.
   - **init_from keeps the epoch count:** a test run ended immediately because init_from continues battle3's epoch (5829). Used `--epochs 5843`.
   - **Inaccurate stop message:** "(no row)" was wrong, since the BattleAI round's row was still written. Reworded.
   - **Merge conflict:** merging origin/main (0239df9) conflicted in docs/performance; resolved with `--ours`. The merge commit's tree equals its parent's.
   - **My own overstatement:** I had said items 2, 3 and 4 were worth doing; measurement showed they were small, and I told the user so.

5. Problem Solving (results):
   - **MPS env var A/B** (5 min warm-up + 10 min measured): battle updates 26 vs 26; campaign updates took 28.4 vs 23.8 ms per decision; swap-in 12.4 → 0.6 MB/s; compression 2.0 → 1.75 GB/s. A modest gain.
   - **Battle client** (bench_ab: 60 battles, 4,328 decisions, both builds identical): 41.87 → 34.39 M instructions per decision (−18%); peak footprint 794 → 501 MB. Replays: 0 of 107.8M + 153.6M values differ.
   - **Dropped ideas:** handing memory back between battles (malloc_zone_pressure_relief) only took mean RSS 231 → 208 MB, so it was reverted. For campaign records, zlib level 1 is already the best zlib setting (0.84 ms per decision on 853 KB raw).
   - **CPU inference profile** (batch 1, 7.1 ms): facts 3.5 ms (distance_maps 1.19, threat_maps 0.70), action heads 1.39, trunk 0.89. So item 4 was not done.
   - **Eval determinism:** 41 values identical idle vs loaded (12.6 vs 17.3 s).
   - **End-to-end eval test:** 3 epochs trained during an eval; SIGTERM ended the run cleanly in 6 s with no leftovers.
   - **Pushes:**
     - vcmi alpha_heroes: e1be37e4a → 3102490f2.
     - main: d7a92a2 → 0239df9 (my perf commit alone).
     - Then 0239df9 → 4036691, which includes b1's commits f3e5b1d, 05914d0, 3581018, 16e50ce, 626419d plus my 84aa3cd, f0b5c75 and the no-op merge.
   - **Cleanup:** the worktree .worktrees/perf and branch perf2 were removed; runtime symlinks are intact; nothing of mine is running.

     - The worker connects to the host given in --master for all ports, so SSH tunnels work.
     - Token in runtime/remote-token; contracts check via remote.contracts() (battle 7a03de8ce5feadca, campaign 63c0862c95d36a97).
     - A worker without --once reconnects every 10 s; the master never waits on a worker; per-connection threads; no TCP keepalives.

     - Battle updates ~170/h → 500–700/h.
     - Campaign updates ~18/h → 55–90/h (estimate).

     - The Mac played ~49 of ~215 battles, latency-bound at 16 ms.
     - No leftovers.
   - **Connectivity analysis:** the master continues without workers and workers reconnect. Weak spots:
     - silent drops (~15 min to notice without keepalives);
     - the tunnel doesn't restart itself;
     - Mac sleep (use caffeinate).
   - **Keep-alive:**
     - Needed: since the 18:04 WSL restart no terminal holds a WSL session.

   - Why b1's changes were right: Adam's scale invariance is steady-state only; a 25× gradient-scale jump with β2 0.999 inflates steps up to ~6× for hundreds of steps; |m|/√v falls with β1 whatever the signal; the 0.8-memory variances carry V_end's batch spread.
   - AlphaStar (Vinyals et al. 2019): entity transformer; scatter connections = entity embeddings → 1D conv to 32 channels + ReLU → scattered into a map layer at each unit's (x,y), concatenated with minimap planes for the ResNet spatial encoder; deep LSTM; autoregressive heads + pointer network; critic with the opponent's observations (training only); supervised init + KL to the supervised policy; z strategy statistics with pseudo-rewards (active 25%); V-trace (policy) + TD(λ) (value) + UPGO (bootstrap with V when the behaviour action was worse than average, one-step Q = r + V); league: main agents (35% SP, 50% PFSP, 15% forgotten), main exploiters, league exploiters, PFSP f_hard = (1−x)^p, f_var = x(1−x), snapshots every 2e9 steps, exploiter resets to supervised. Fig. 3 numbers: F architecture (supervised, Protoss vs All, vs Elite bot) 0/7/36/71/87% (baseline, +action delays, +pointer net, +transformer, +scatter); E human data Elo 149/936/1020/1400/1540 (none, supervised, human init, +supervised KL, +statistics); A league Elo 1540/1693/1824; B RPP 6/35/62%; I off-policy avg win-rate V-trace 49 → +TD(λ) 73 → +UPGO 82; K value function without/with opponent info 22% → 82%; fine-tuning on MMR>6200 replays 87% → 96% PvP; naive self-play high Elo but forgetful (C/D).
   - OpenAI Five (Berner et al. 2019): 4096-unit LSTM shared across 5 heroes, Process Set modules with shared weights, cross-hero pool (maxpool first 25% of the vector across replicas), target unit via attention over unit embeddings masked per action, value as a linear projection of the LSTM state (shares the network, no hidden info; AlphaStar's privileged critic cited as "a promising direction"); PPO clip 0.2, GAE λ 0.95, γ 0.9993 (horizon H = T/(1−γ)), horizon 180 → 360 s (840 final), team spirit r_i = (1−τ)ρ_i + τρ̄ with τ 0.3 → 0.8 (1.0 final; lower τ cuts gradient variance early), entropy coefficient 0.01 → 0.001 (0.01 best; 0 early does not prevent learning), lr 5e-5 → 5e-6, Adam 0.9/0.999, batch ~983k timesteps (16-frame unrolls), 32 PPO steps per iteration, value loss weight 1.0, rewards normalized by a running std, shaped rewards × 0.6^(T/10 min), zero-sum by subtracting the enemy's mean; 80% self-play / 20% past versions sampled ∝ e^{q_i}, snapshot every 10 iterations initialized at max q, q_i ← q_i − η N p_i (η 0.01) when the current agent wins; staleness (M−N versions): ~8 versions = significant slowdown, target 0–1; sample reuse 2–3 → ~2× slowdown, 8 may prevent learning, target ~1; surgery = function-preserving weight changes, lr 0 for several hours after, past opponents must be migrated identically; Rerun from scratch with the final code: 2 months, ~20% of Five's compute, >98% win vs the final Five, the surgered model "plateaued at a weaker skill level"; environment randomization (initial state, Roshan health 0..full, hero lineup, item builds; lane assignments unnecessary); scripted item/skill builds (macro actions); zero-team-spirit-embedding anecdote.

   - PPO update (campaign_model/train.py ppo_update): prepass (no grad) collects anchor logp, end_now, growth_now (G7 logit), value_now (V7 logit), log_phi, side, state_all (value_hidden), logits_all; privileged corrections corr_all (PrivilegedCritic); with privileged_credit value_now += corr[:, 2] and end_now = sigmoid(end_logit + corr[:, 3]). Schedule credit, verbatim:
     ```python
     if config.credit == "schedule" and growth_real is not None:
         real7 = torch.from_numpy(np.asarray(growth_real, dtype=np.float32)[:, -1]).to(device)
         grow = (real7 - log_phi).clamp(-GROWTH_CAP, GROWTH_CAP) - (torch.sigmoid(growth_now) * 2 * GROWTH_CAP - GROWTH_CAP)
         real_change = torch.from_numpy(np.asarray(targets, dtype=np.float32)[:, -1]).to(device) * 2 * SHARE_CHANGE_CAP - SHARE_CHANGE_CAP
         change = real_change - share_baseline(real_change, torch.sigmoid(value_now) * 2 * SHARE_CHANGE_CAP - SHARE_CHANGE_CAP, share_trust)
         day = torch.tensor([d.day for d in decisions], dtype=torch.float32, device=device)
         w = share_weight(day, side, config)
         advantage = w * change + (1 - w) * grow  # (nan where a label is unknown: no credit)
     if end is not None and end["weight"] > 0:
         turn = choices(decisions)["kind"] == c.DecisionKind.TURN
         later = end_advantages(end_now.cpu().numpy(), end["step"], end["week"], end["real"], end["price"], turn, config.decision_price, end["outcome"])
         ...
         if attributed is not None or config.credit == "schedule":
             advantage = torch.from_numpy(blend_credit(standardized(advantage.cpu().numpy()), standardized(later), end["weight"])).to(device)
     credited = torch.isfinite(advantage); advantage = (advantage - mean) / (std + 1e-8) over credited
     ```
     `share_baseline(real_change, predicted, trust) = trust * predicted + (1 - trust) * mean(real_change)`; `share_weight = clamp(start + per_day × (36/side) × day, 0, 1)`; `end_advantages`: `there = values[step] if step >= 0 else real; td = there - values - price_if_turn; later = values[week] or real; weekly = later - values - price; result = where(isfinite(weekly), (1 - outcome) * td + outcome * weekly, td)`; `blend_credit(seven, end, weight)` = (1−weight)·seven + weight·end, a nan part adding nothing. In learner.step: `end["weight"] = attributed_end_weight (0.2) if weight > 0` for schedule (the horizon mode would use end_credit_max × trust_Vend = 0.88); `end["outcome"] = 1 − 0.75 × trust_Vend`; `share_trust=self.trust()` (max(trust_floor 0.4, EMA of min(value_ev)/0.4) from the PUBLIC recorded V5/V7); `starts = day_starts(self.model, played, lock)` gives per (color, day) first turn decision: (index, shares [V2,V5,V7 sigmoid], grown [G2,G5,G7 log growth], log_value, V_end, prior) with the current model; `end["step"]`/`end["week"]` are mapped to this update's row numbers. 4. Errors and fixes:
   - A foreground `sleep 45; tail` was blocked by the harness → used an `until … do sleep 5; done` loop to wait for the background job.

   - b4.py counted 882 moves onto own towns while b9.py counted 1,563 town entries → explained: b4 classified a town tile holding another of our heroes as a meeting.
   - Accuracy correction to carry forward: the reported "end head explains 82% of the outcome" is plain EV (inflated by the player-count prior); the right figure over the prior is 0.35 (head_ev). "Growth head 54%" and "privileged V7 fits twice as well as public (0.30 vs 0.15)" stand.
   - User feedback on my report: do not hand them decisions that are not real tradeoffs ("STOP saying 'your call' …"); several of my fix directions were "stupid" or incomplete: the day-based exclusion and the repeat prior do not answer "what about tomorrow?"; forcing the exchange label to follow NK2's plan must not remove the ability to learn other exchanges (they suggest an NK2-like exchange as a multi action that can also be declined); a metric is fine.

5. Problem Solving:
   - Solved: full audit of the campaign learning path with real data; root cause of the meeting loop located (stateless per-decision re-planning of Nullkiller2 + the exchange carried out by our MULTI outside the teacher's span with a destination rule that differs from the plan's); credit weakness quantified; several suspects cleared (Φ consistency, logp/sampling, label alignment, battle path).
   - Open (the pending answer): design the actual fix for the meeting loop and the step credit, precise enough for a weaker AI to implement. Working ideas so far, NOT yet verified against Nullkiller2's code and not approved by the user:
     1. Meeting loop: (i) check whether real NK2 locks a chain's heroes for the rest of its turn (HERO_CHAIN lock in ExecuteHeroChain::accept, cleared at each makeTurn) and what direction rule AIGateway::heroExchangeStarted uses; if so, the advice loses exactly that per-turn state, and the fix is to keep it: AlphaMapAI remembers which heroes completed an exchange this turn and re-applies them as locks to the advising NK2 before each `makeTurn` (small labeled patch in our NK2 fork, like keepPlan), so its first request becomes its next real task; (ii) make the exchange consistent with the plan that asked for it while keeping freedom: the receiver = the plan's chain actor when the meeting was the teacher's advice (fallback: today's level/strength rule), accept MULTI TAKE_BEST into that receiver as the NK2-like exchange and bring MULTI inside the exchange span so the other MULTIs are weighed against it (soft cross-entropy, never a mask; RL credit can still prefer another exchange); (iii) "tomorrow": NK2 asks again only if it still wants the army moved, at most once a pair a day, and the step credit prices the wasted movement; (iv) a repeat-meeting and own-hero/town movement metric in training and eval; (v) the town-visit loop (GATHER dropped from the taught set after the emptied-towns collapse) needs its own look.
     2. Step credit for the schedule credit: per decision, the change of the critic's own forecast to the player's next turn decision the same day, for growth F_g = log Φ + G7's forecast and for share V7 (privileged), mixed with the 7-day outcome term as `(1 − s)·step + s·outcome` with s = 1 − (1 − outcome_share_min) × trust(head) (G7 → 0.25, V7 → ~0.5), the day's last decision keeping the outcome term, and the step alone where the outcome label is missing (last 7 days); noise falls roughly 4–12× at the same expected signal because the outcome noise is weighted by s²; uses `end["step"]`, `day`, `growth_now`, `value_now`, `log_phi` already in ppo_update, new arguments for the two outcome shares from `learner.grade_trust()`; behind a config switch, bit-identical when off; advantage code is outside the CUDA graphs. Explanation to give: a decision's outcome advantage = shared 7-day result minus the baseline at that decision; within a day the only differences are the baseline's drift before the decision, which says nothing about the decision itself.
     3. Smaller issues: privileged baseline trust from the privileged V7's own pooled EV (or max with the public trust); when the loss shares are frozen, scale the frozen teacher weight by the odds of `teacher_share_for(score)` over the odds at 0.6 so it falls once the eval passes 0.6; no decision price needed once the step credit exists.

     - Step credit, replacing b1's function (b1's two tests still pass because the new arguments default to the old behaviour):
       ```python
       STEP_CLASS_PRIOR = 50.0  # rows at 0 added to each class's mean rest (step_credit)

       def step_classes(kind, action):
           return (action + 1).clamp(min=0) + (kind == c.DecisionKind.EXCHANGE).long() * (c.N_ACTION_TYPES + 1)

       def forecast_trust(real, forecast, full_ev):
           known = torch.isfinite(real) & torch.isfinite(forecast)
           if int(known.sum()) < 2: return 0.0
           spread = float(real[known].var())
           if spread < 1e-12: return 0.0
           explained = 1.0 - float((real[known] - forecast[known]).pow(2).mean()) / spread
           return float(min(1.0, max(0.0, explained / full_ev)))

       def forecast_steps(forecast, step, day):
           j = step.clamp(min=0)
           same = (step >= 0) & (day[j] == day)
           return torch.where(same, forecast[j] - forecast, torch.full_like(forecast, float("nan")))

       STEP_REPORT = (("move", 0, MOVE), ("travel", 0, TRAVEL), ("build", 0, BUILD), ("recruit", 0, RECRUIT), ("upgrade", 0, UPGRADE),
           ("hire", 0, HIRE), ("trade", 0, TRADE), ("multi", 0, MULTI), ("exchange_multi", 1, MULTI), ("exchange_army", 1, ARMY))
       def step_report(moved, kind, action): ...  # {step_<name>: mean own step} floats

       def step_credit(term, forecast, step, day, trust, classes=None, price=None, outcome_share_min=0.25):
           s = 1.0 - (1.0 - outcome_share_min) * float(min(1.0, max(0.0, trust)))
           moved = forecast_steps(forecast, step, day)
           stepped = torch.isfinite(moved)
           both = torch.isfinite(term) & stepped
           rest = torch.where(both, term - moved, torch.zeros_like(term))
           correction = torch.zeros_like(term)
           if classes is not None and int(both.sum()):
               count = int(classes.max()) + 1
               sums = torch.zeros(count, ...).index_add_(0, classes[both], rest[both])
               rows = torch.zeros(count, ...).index_add_(0, classes[both], torch.ones_like(rest[both]))
               correction = (sums / (rows + STEP_CLASS_PRIOR))[classes]
           own = moved - price if price is not None else moved
           return torch.where(both, own + s * rest + (1 - s) * correction, torch.where(stepped, own + correction, term))
       ```

     ```python
     def actions_ahead(game, days=c.VALUE_OFFSETS[-1]):
         out, by = np.zeros(len(game.decisions), dtype=np.float32), {}
         for i, d in enumerate(game.decisions):
             by.setdefault(d.color, []).append(i)
         for rows in by.values():
             day = np.array([game.decisions[i].day for i in rows])
             acts = np.array([game.decisions[i].action_type >= 0 and game.decisions[i].action_type != c.ActionType.END for i in rows], dtype=np.float64)
             total = np.concatenate([[0.0], np.cumsum(acts)])
             out[rows] = total[np.searchsorted(day, day + days, side="right")] - total[: len(rows)]
         return out
     ```

   - **scripts/headless.sh** (0c8ebfd, deployed in 46): Linux stall sample now adds, per stalled process, `ss -x -a -p -n` lines for each of its socket inodes (Recv-Q/Send-Q on both ends, owning process) and each peer process's threads counted by `comm` (digits → N), state and wchan:
     ```bash
     if command -v ss > /dev/null; then
         SOCKETS=$(ss -x -a -p -n 2>/dev/null)
         PEERS=""
         for INODE in $(ls -l /proc/"$P"/fd 2>/dev/null | sed -n 's/.*socket:\[\([0-9]*\)\].*/\1/p'); do
             ENDS=$(echo "$SOCKETS" | grep -E "(^| )$INODE( |\$)" | tr -s ' ')
             echo "$ENDS"
             PEERS="$PEERS $(echo "$ENDS" | grep -o 'pid=[0-9]*' | cut -d= -f2 | tr '\n' ' ')"
         done
         for PEER in $(echo $PEERS | tr ' ' '\n' | sort -un); do
             [ "$PEER" = "$P" ] && continue
             echo "peer pid $PEER: $(tr '\0' ' ' < "/proc/$PEER/cmdline" 2>/dev/null | cut -c1-200)"
             for TASK in /proc/"$PEER"/task/*; do
                 echo "$(sed 's/[0-9][0-9]*/N/' "$TASK/comm" 2>/dev/null) state $(sed 's/.*) //' "$TASK/stat" 2>/dev/null | cut -d' ' -f1) wchan $(cat "$TASK/wchan" 2>/dev/null)"
             done | sort | uniq -c | sort -rn
         done
     fi
     ```

At accumulate 24 the 4 steps of an update are 4 passes over the same decisions, so m holds 4 copies of one gradient: weight of the current update (1−β1)(1+β1+β1²+β1³) = 0.344, earlier updates discounted by 0.656 each; pure noise gives Var(m) = 0.2077σ², rms |m|/sqrt(v) 0.456, median 0.31 (independent steps: 0.155). 2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Schema 17 / further windows (built, NOT deployed):** MOVE's table is [8 window slots × 33 × 33]. Before: each hero's window = square on its own level; MOVE legal only for tiles reached today in it; TRAVEL only areas a day or more away, so tiles past a gate reachable today needed a MOVE onto the gate (priced at 0). Now: each hero that can move gets ONE further window (config `campaign.further_windows`, 1) in a window slot no hero holds: a square on the level holding the most of its today-reachable tiles not shown by its own window, around the soonest reached there, center clamped onto the map. Inputs `w_extra` (HO,) = hero token + 1 (0 = slot's own hero) and `w_extra_pos` (HO, 3), appended at the end (older records read 0). A tile is legal only in the first of a hero's windows that shows it. Rejected the "reach-ordered list of 1,089 tiles" because window science needs neighbours and unreachable tiles (worst_guard, next_to_monster, unexplored_around, blocked_by_guards).
   - **Unseen exits:** VCMI's pathfinder goes through a teleporter only to an exit the player sees (`getVisibleTeleportObjects`). `TILE_UNSEEN_EXIT = 512` (beyond N_TILE_FLAGS bits) in w_flags/tile_flags, `OBJECT_UNSEEN_EXIT = 8` in m_flags; MOVE's `explore` = max(unexplored_around, flag) (not whirlpools, obj 111); TRAVEL's area hidden = max(hidden, `features.unseen_exits`); tile encoder parameter `encode_tiles.unseen_exit` starts at 0 (model.ADDED). `deadChannels` set from showTeleportDialog's impassable. Config `campaign.unseen_exits` (True).
   - Switches reach games as env `ALPHA_MAP_FURTHER_WINDOWS`, `ALPHA_MAP_UNSEEN_EXITS` via `play.window_settings(config)` (supply.campaign_settings, evaluate.py). With both off the model is bit-identical to e577a76 (42 outputs/features, difference 0).
   - **Measurements for schema 17:** NK2 game 40 days 36×36×2: 43% of hero-turns have tiles in reach today outside own window (median 31 vs 77 inside); 2nd/3rd further windows median 8/3 tiles (hence cap 1). Our AI with NK2 advice: 16 moves through a further window, all with effect, 14 of 14 followed ended on target's level; 18 of 865 advised MOVE labels and 32 of 1,192 accepted entries in a further window. Client CPU 4.4–4.7 ms/decision with vs 4.5–4.6 without; CPU forward 27.2 ms at 3 windows, +1.6 ms a window. **Correction needed to the user:** I said the PC's GPU inference computes all 8 slots so sees no change; the run config has `campaign.inference_device=cpu inference_processes=12`, so the PC's campaign inference is on CPU and each further window costs ~+6% of a forward there. 2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **GATHER collapse explained:** v2728's heroes were already killed on the enemy's turn (~18 per 12 games vs Nullkiller2, ~5k army each, since 80-90% of the army sat in garrisons). GATHER advice moved half the army onto heroes in ~10 updates; same death rate, 20-43k each; empty towns behind. Survivors scored higher with GATHER (0.60-0.80 vs 0.56-0.63). The readings right after the v2735 restart were normal post-restart spikes; real rise from v2744, clear at v2749.
   - **Value-loss guard** (`config.value_jump_stop` 3.0): median of the last 7 fresh readings over the lowest 50-median among the 300 before; at/above the factor the learner holds campaign updates (drops their games, FAILURE lines, file `campaign/held`, TensorBoard `campaign/value_jump`); a restart lifts it; judged only after 7 own readings. Fires on joint7's history only at v1137 (2026-11-05) and v2749.
   - **Schema 17** (further windows + unseen exits) deployed inert (`campaign.further_windows=0 campaign.unseen_exits=false`).
   - **Run-away prior (b1, schema 18):** new input `travel_stop [HO, L, TG, TG]` = (z×256 + y)×256 + x + 1 (where today's walk toward each TRAVEL area stops; old records read 0); `features.night_danger`, `sheltered_danger`, `shelter_open` (a town shelters its garrison hero, its visitor when no garrison hero, or a hero that can get in), `travel_stops`, `signed_offer` (MOVE's/TRAVEL's type offers go below 0 when every move is worse than staying); `features.night_gain` (in model.ADDED, starts at gain 1); night term = (P lost tonight here − at the day's end tile) × (army + hero + replacement) in MOVE's and TRAVEL's LOGIT priors only (network inputs untouched: `tile_prior` = logits, `travel_logits`; `travel_prior` unchanged as the network's input).
   - **The model already "knows":** foresight "hero" head (still ours tomorrow; trained every turn decision) AUC 0.86 (v2728) / 0.93 (v2830) for tonight's deaths; hand-made MOVE danger AUC 0.87/0.90; calibration: danger 0.8-1.0 → 68-77% killed, inside a town ~0. `danger_gain` raw −0.147 = 0.37 of prior. `travel_prior` and `end_logit` had no danger term.

     ```python
     VALUE_JUMP_WINDOW, VALUE_JUMP_BASELINE, VALUE_JUMP_MEMORY = 7, 50, 300
     def value_jump(readings, window=..., baseline=..., memory=...):  # -> (ratio, now, level)
         readings = [float(r) for r in readings if r == r]
         if len(readings) < window + baseline: return nan, nan, nan
         last, before = readings[-window:], readings[-window - memory : -window]
         level = min(float(np.median(before[i : i + baseline])) for i in range(len(before) - baseline + 1))
         now = float(np.median(last))
         return (now / level if level > 0 else nan), now, level
     def fresh_readings(progress, keep=VALUE_JUMP_MEMORY + VALUE_JUMP_WINDOW)  # progress.csv's value_loss_fresh
     ```

   - **Feature plan v3 (approved by "ok. Make the feature changes"):** every new number is a channel on an existing (hero, tile) window pair or (hero, area) TRAVEL pair; value counted six bounded ways everywhere (`g/(|g|+2k)`, `g/(|g|+20k)`, share of empire `s/(|s|+0.02)`, `s/(|s|+0.2)`, days of own growth `p/(|p|+1)`, `p/(|p|+7)`, p = g / daily gain over 7 days floored at 0.5% of Φ, needs the model's own Φ of 7 days ago passed back); time as two features (own fractional days; baseline days = movement points/1,500), each as discounts `1/(1+t)`, `1/(1+t/3)`; value per day `g/(t+0.25)`; gaps `-tanh(gap/4)`; NK2 priority `-tanh(ln(best/priority)/3)` vs the decision's best and the hero's best; danger one-sided `r²/(1+r²)`; path loss as share and `ln(1-loss+0.01)`; kind one-hot; settled `1-1/(1+n)`; other-hero-headed-here; first step carries its destination's pair vector; plan vector = preference-weighted mean of destination tile embeddings on the hero token; β prior on NK2's preference (β by paired test 0/2/4, β_fallback smaller for gather-tier fallback entries); known-direction features get sign-fixed bounded gains with a small start (0..2 from 0.25), unknown-direction zero-init; plan head stays and is fed back; NK2 inputs dropped in a share of training games; gates: "readable" (≤64-weight readout from the prior must beat 1.51 and put NK2's label first on ≥70% of TRAVEL/MOVE-object decisions with a candidate) and "no explosion, nothing dead".
   - **Schema 19 (b1's final field list; ints only, "none" = 0 with +1 shifts; TAIL_STARTS gains "w_arrive_left"):** `w_arrive_left` (HO,W,W) movement left on arrival +1 (0 none); `g_phi_past` (gold, rounded), `g_phi_past_days` (7 or oldest 1..6, 0 none), `g_nk_present` bool, `g_nk_tier` (1..6, 0 none), `g_nk_count`; candidates NK=128 (NK_PER_HERO=16): `k_hero` (slot+1, 0 empty), `k_dest` (NK,3), `k_dest_cell` (slot×W×W+cell +1), `k_dest_area` (hero×AREAS+area +1), `k_move` (first step's MOVE entry +1: destination's own entry if legal today else today's stop; never another path tile), `k_travel` (TRAVEL entry +1), `k_score` (NK,2) priority×100 at the winning tier and at EXPLORE_AND_GATHER, `k_days` ×1000, `k_loss` ×10000, `k_danger` ×1000 (clamped 1e6), `k_kind` 0 visit/capture,1 enemy hero,2 town,3 defend,4 meet our hero,5 explore,6 other, `k_settled`, `k_flags` (1 arrives, 2 our pathfinder can't reach, 4 teleporter, 8 chain); `t_nk_threat` (TO,3), `h_nk_threat` (HO,3): has, turns, ratio×1000; output `phi` float32. b1's decisions: advisor runs at every turn decision when env `ALPHA_MAP_NK2_INPUTS=1` (independent of ALPHA_MAP_NK2_ADVICE); exchanges/questions: present false; dropout per game in supply.py via `config.nk2_input_dropout`; evals/paired evals: inputs on; g_phi_past kept per player in the client (ring of 7).
   - **Model structure (read for the integration):** `CampaignModelV2.forward`: domain_knowledge → encode → take_sides → join → see → think → foresee → think_again → decide. Pair vector `pairs()` = pair_hero(hero)+pair_tile(cell)+pair_reach(window_reach)+pair_fight+pair_strength [K,W,W,dp]; `state.window_reach = self.reach(None, reach_features(...))` feeds both pair_reach and the tile reader's bias; MOVE: `move_known` → `move_mix`/`move_mix_direct`/`move_pair` → `move_out`; TRAVEL: `travel_hero + travel_area + travel_days(E.amount(days))` → `travel_pair(cat(travel, tanh(prior/4)))` → `travel_out`; priors `known.tile_prior`, `known.travel_logits`; `known.tile_value` [K,W,W] gold; windows packed via `windows_of` (known.windows flat decision×HO+slot, known.n, known.real; fixed windows for CUDA graphs), `dense()`, `real_only()`, `map_index(positions, b, side)`, `ft.window_heroes`, `ft.slot_heroes`. Checkpoint compatibility: `model.ADDED` (named params → zeros), `ADDED_HEADS` (prefixes → loading model's init), `GROWN_INPUTS` + `pad_new_inputs` (new input columns at 0), `zero_output()`, `ft.bounded(raw, low, high, start, step)` sign-fixed gains decayed toward start; `Features` dict (`f.travel_prior, f.travel_logits = self.travel_prior(x, f)`).
   - **Integration design I settled on (not yet coded):** add new zero-output modules onto existing vectors instead of growing existing layers' inputs: `time_reach` (4 discounts → dc, added to window_reach); `view_pair` (zero-init Linear(NV, dp)) added inside `pairs()` and a `view_mix` → logit for MOVE (value views 6, rates 3+3, gaps 2, candidate channels 25, destination's own value views); destination pair gather via `move_dest_cell`/`travel_dest_cell` with an inverse map from (decision, slot) to packed window index, through zero-init `toward` projections; TRAVEL views into `travel` before `travel_pair` (expose `f.travel_gold = value` in model.py's travel_prior); plan vector at the end of `see` (state.tiles gathered at candidates' destinations via map_index, weighted by plan_weight, scatter-added per hero, zero-init `plan_in`); threats/global in `join` (zero-init); prior: MOVE/TRAVEL prior += β×gain×prior + β_f×gain_f×prior_fallback plus sign-fixed gains on log survival and risk, all scaled so β=0 is inert; β stored as saved buffers `features.nk_beta`, `features.nk_beta_fallback` (ADDED → 0 for old checkpoints) with env override `ALPHA_NK2_PRIOR`, `ALPHA_NK2_PRIOR_FALLBACK` for paired tests; `x.get` guards so a missing input means absent.
   - **PC step facts (read-only, last 40 updates):** learning_rate 1e-5 with lr_scale_max 1.0 (lr at its cap); last_kl median 0.021 vs target 0.03; shares of the shared gradient: teacher 0.65, policy 0.21, value 0.075, foresight 0.043 (plan label ≈ 80% of foresight's); loss weights value 352.59, teacher 2.0317, foresight 0.6956; teacher_agree 0.249; teacher_ce 1.19. With the teacher off Adam's second moments shrink within ~20 updates and the policy's own step would grow ~3x up to the KL target: plan = set `target_kl` at the teacher-off deploy from a measured teacher-off update (in the deploy's GPU check) so the policy's own step stays what it is.
   - **Credit facts for the "next issue":** `credit: "schedule"` = w×(share's 7-day surprise) + (1−w)×(growth's), w = 0.1+0.02×day; ~98% of the advantage's variance is shared by a player-day (2026-11-09 audit); `train.step_credit` exists but the user set it aside on 2026-11-09 ("I do not want any value on actual steps, only on day+2/5/7"); `train.loss_noise` measures gradient noise scale per term; critic explains 12-18% of share change.
   - **GATHER ramp monitoring:** army_on_heroes 0.14 → 0.29 (v3104, weight 0.036); eliminated10 0.008-0.021 (stop 0.04); deep-end P(penalized entry) series: v2862 0.038, v2912 0.076, v3003 0.084, v3025 0.088, v3045 0.090, v3069 0.090, v3090 0.096 (trigger for `nk2_night_drop` test ~0.10; creep began before the ramp). PC evals: v3025 0.533/10, v3045 0.497/12, v3069 0.466/12, v3090 0.498/11 (single evals ±0.11).

     candidate_tables logic: valid rows; winning = score_w>0; fallback = gather-scored rows of a hero with nothing at the winning tier; shadow = other gather-scored rows; key = 3+p_all (winning) / 2+g_all (fallback) / 1+g_all (shadow) / 0; one best candidate per entry found with pairwise [B,NK,NK] comparisons (ties: earlier row) and placed with `scatter_add` (no scatter_reduce, no nonzero); mass = summed live preference squashed m/(m+1); `others` = best live preference of another hero's candidate with the same destination; plan_weight = exp(4 × (g_hero if fallback else p_hero)) normalised per hero.

4. Errors and fixes:
   - My wrong claims, corrected to the user: recommending against NK2's numbers as inputs (reversed after the follow test and readout fits: "You were right about feeding its numbers in; I was wrong"); own-computed candidate features (closest ratio, TRAVEL from tile values, plan feedback) as the fix (dead as linear readouts on the policy); "empty-tile labels are steps beyond the window" (97.5% inside); "~0.65 ceiling" for the plan head (b1's correction: not a bound); teleport gap "~8%" (9-15% of priority mass); loss/std² for the valve (not like for like: value_loss_fresh is an excess cross-entropy mixing heads); "we are at parity" (we are ~0.5 where NK2 = 1.0).
   - User feedback: first feature plan "is not a 'plan'" (raw fields, no representation, no connection to hero/tile embeddings; days as int or embedding bad); on plan v2: time needs two features (own days and movement points over a baseline hero's speed); value on arrival and value per movement points; "multiple bound ways to count value everywhere"; strength log ratio "can explode" (pixies vs a strong hero); path loss log OK if bounded with a tiny gradient and no wrong-sign weight from initialization; "I want learning to be smooth and fast". Earlier: valves "kinda hacky"; the user interrupted the overhaul to ask for plans first.
   - b1 overwrote my memory file `teacher-question-2026-11-11.md`; I rewrote it from context.
   - Fetching checkpoint_03069.pt failed at the run folder's top level (file lives in `<run>/campaign/`); fixed the path.
   - views.py test failures: `clamp` zeroes autograd at exactly 0 and 1 for log_survival (changed the test to probe 0.001/0.5/0.999 and check the end values apart); tolerance failures from the wire's rounding of scores (loosened to 1e-3/1e-2); a leftover `if False else` hack in `best_of` removed; plan weights of "shadow" candidates fixed (`torch.where(fallback, g_hero, p_hero)`).
   - gate_channels.py: removed a duplicated assignment; binary channels exempted from the flatness test.
   - Replay under CPU load ran ~33-35 min a replicate instead of ~13; verified games were not cut by time caps (early stops are eliminations, 12-13 a replicate as before).
   - A grep on the PC log matched old traceback lines when looking for lr_scale (lr_scale is not a CSV column); concluded from config (learning_rate 1e-5, lr_scale_max 1.0) and last_kl below target that lr sits at the cap.

5. Problem Solving:
   - Established with b1 that NK2's knowledge is worth ~+0.29 in our own action space, that the teacher's loss (60-65% of the shared gradient, agreement 0.24) is an inefficient way to get it, and that the user's design (NK2's per-action scores as inputs + a dense plan) is the right core; produced plan v3 with representation rules.
   - Established the valves' premise is contradicted by the collapse's numbers; decided valves off at the next deploy.
   - Built and tested the Python view/candidate-table layer and the teacher-off switch; designed the model integration; identified the Adam/KL consequence of removing the teacher and the remedy (target_kl from a measured teacher-off update).
   - Ongoing: b1 building C++ export + schema 19 (estimate ~15:00 for the export, schema, records and a 2-game smoke); my model integration not yet coded (waiting on b1's schema commit for tests, but can be coded against the field list with test-injected inputs); gates and β paired tests not yet run; deploy not prepared; GATHER ramp still live and creeping (army on heroes 0.29).
